   - R's immediate neighbors are N, O, C, and J.
   - I's immediate neighbors are H, J, and Q.

   - From R, we can go to O, then to C, then to G, then to J, and finally to I.
   - From R, we can go to N, then to C, then to G, then to J, and finally to I.
   - From R, we can go directly to C, then to G, then to J, and finally to I.
   - From R, we can go directly to J, and then to I.
4. **Identify the critical edges:**
   - To disconnect R and I, we need to cut off all paths between them. The critical edges are those that connect nodes in different components of these paths.
   - The edges (R, J) and (J, I) form a direct path. Removing either of these edges will disrupt this direct connection.
   - The edges (R, C) and (C, G) form another path. Removing either of these edges will disrupt this connection.
   - The edges (R, N) and (N, C) form another path. Removing either of these edges will disrupt this connection.
   - The edges (G, J) and (J, I) form another path. Removing either of these edges will disrupt this connection.
5. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - By removing the edges (R, J) and (J, I), we disrupt all possible paths between R and I. These two edges form a minimal cut set.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes is 2.

**Answer:**

   - J is connected to Q, P, H, C, and R.

   - From J: J-H (H is connected to B which connects to E), J-C (C is connected to S which connects to E), J-R (R is connected to B which connects to E).

   - E can reach J via E-F-B-R-J.
   - E can reach J via E-B-R-J.
   - J can reach E via J-H-B-E.
   - J can reach E via J-C-S-E.
   - J can reach E via J-R-B-E.
5. **Bottlenecks**: Since there are multiple paths between E and J, we need to remove edges to cut all possible paths.
6. **Optimal Cut Set**:

     - Remove E-B to cut E's direct connection to B.
     - Remove J-R to cut J's direct connection to R.
     - Remove C-S to cut the other paths between E and J via S and C (since these nodes also connect indirectly).

To solve the task, let's analyze the given graph image step-by-step to determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and N).

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are E and N.

2. **Identify Immediate Neighboring Nodes:**
   - For node E: The immediate neighboring nodes are B, F, and S.
   - For node N: The immediate neighboring nodes are B, H, G, O, and C.

3. **Map Out All Connecting Paths Between Black Nodes:**
   - **Paths via B:**
     - E -> B -> N
     - E -> B -> H -> N
     - E -> B -> T -> N
   - **Paths via F:**
     - E -> F -> C -> N
     - E -> F -> C -> O -> N
     - E -> F -> G -> N
   - **Paths via S:**
     - There is no path from S to N in this graph directly besides via B.

4. **Evaluate Bottlenecks (minimum edges to disconnect E and N):**
   - **Path via B:**
     - Removing the edge (E, B) disconnects multiple paths that go through B.
   - **Path via F:**
     - Removing the edge (F, C) disconnects multiple paths that go through F and C.
   - **Path via G:**
     - Removing both (F, G) and (G, N) cuts off the connection through G.

### Calculate Minimum Edges to Remove:

There are multiple paths, and we are looking for the minimal set of edges to remove:
1. Remove (E, B) and (F, C):
   - This will disconnect the paths without leaving any alternative connection that wasn't removed.
2. Finally, ensure to double-check that no other connecting paths exist.

Hence, the minimum number of edges to remove is determined by the following:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, B), (F, C)]

   - The black node A is connected to the nodes B, E, S, F, and O.
   - The black node I is connected to the nodes Q, J, H, R, G, T, and C.

2. **Identify Common Nodes:**
   - Nodes connected to both A and I directly or indirectly must be analyzed to find the minimal cut set.

3. **Analyze Paths:**
   - Paths from A to I via common neighbors must be traced.
   - From A: B → E → Q → I
   - From A: E → I
   - From A: S → T → I
   - From A: F → C → I
   - From A: O → G → I

4. **Bottlenecks and Minimal Cuts:**
   - The critical paths are:
     - A → B → E → Q → I
     - A → E → I
     - A → S → T → I
     - A → F → C → I
     - A → O → G → I

   This reveals that nodes **B, E, and C** are central to multiple paths connecting A and I.

5. **Determine the Cut Set:**
   - Removing any of these central nodes (B, E, or C) will disrupt all paths from A to I.
   - Removing one node may not be sufficient since there are multiple paths.
   - Removing **B, E, and C** entirely ensures that all paths from A to I are cut off.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes A and I from each other is:

**Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, E, C]**

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (N and Q), let's analyze the graph step by step:

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - Black nodes: N and Q.

2. **Examine immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - **N's immediate neighbors:** B and C.
   - **Q's immediate neighbors:** B and J.

3. **Find the paths between N and Q:**
   - Q connects to B, which connects to N. This is one path.
   - Q connects to J, which connects to G, which connects to B, which connects to C, which connects to N. This is another path.
   - Q connects to J, which connects to G, which connects to H, which connects to I, which connects to P.

4. **Bottleneck Identification:**
   - To disconnect N and Q, we need to consider the bottlenecks. The bottle necks here are B and J.
   - Removing one edge from N to either B or C cannot disconnect Q yet.
   - Removing the connections between N and Q and B is impossible without leaving a path, because Q connects indirectly to C via B and G. Same thing with B later leading to G and H.
   - Removing edge between J leading to Q leaves no other paths.

5. **Find exploiting more further endpoints:**
   - Removing edge from both C to N and J to Q disconnects both the paths found.

6. **Check minimal removal:**
   - After carefully checking, removing edges between Q-J and  N-C suffices to finally achieve it.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to remove to completely disconnect the black nodes N and Q is:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(J, Q), (N, C)]
--- Ground Truth ---
